People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Now I don’t really want to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself or wanting to be dead?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I don’t have a plan or anything, but I do think sometimes that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move?\n\nPatient: Yeah. My energy is really low. Even if I sit down for a while or get a full night in bed, I still feel worn out—like I’ve been framing a roof all day. Little things, like loading tools into the truck, feel like too much sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t really been tracking it, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing big that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah. People at work have noticed it too. Sometimes I move real slow, like my boots are full of wet cement—takes me forever just to pick up a board or get going. Salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking for the neighbors—even going into my shop just to smell the wood. I used to enjoy all that, and now I just don’t feel like doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s like… everything in the house has gone dull, and I’m just trying not to break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About six months now. It’s been going on long enough that it doesn’t feel like just a rough week or normal stress anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: The pleasure has mostly gone away. I used to look forward to salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking for the neighbors… even just stepping into my shop and smelling the wood. Now I don’t really want to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself or wanting to be dead?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I don’t have a plan or anything, but I do think sometimes that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move?\n\nPatient: Yeah. My energy is really low. Even if I sit down for a while or get a full night in bed, I still feel worn out—like I’ve been framing a roof all day. I haven’t really been tracking it, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing big that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah. People at work have noticed it too. Sometimes I move real slow, like my boots are full of wet cement—takes me forever just to pick up a board or get going. Other times I’m the opposite, pacing around the shop, rubbing my hands, but not actually getting anything done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about yourself or your situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I haven’t heard voices or anything like that. That’s been a real problem. I’ll be looking at blueprints I’ve read my whole life, and it’s like the lines just blur together in my head. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. I know I should understand it right away, but I can’t focus, and I end up asking the younger guys to repeat things I should’ve caught the first time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like everything in the house has lost its color. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess what brings me in is… I’ve stopped wanting to do the things that usually keep me going. Salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking for the neighbors—even going into my shop just to smell the wood. I used to enjoy all that, and now I just don’t feel like doing any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or empty most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It’s like… everything in the house has gone dull, and I’m just trying not to break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: About six months now. It’s been going on long enough that it doesn’t feel like just a rough week or normal stress anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: The pleasure has mostly gone away. I used to look forward to salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking for the neighbors… even just stepping into my shop and smelling the wood. Now I don’t really want to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself or wanting to be dead?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I don’t have a plan or anything, but I do think sometimes that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move?\n\nPatient: Yeah. My energy is really low. Even if I sit down for a while or get a full night in bed, I still feel worn out—like I’ve been framing a roof all day. Little things, like loading tools into the truck, feel like too much sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I haven’t really been tracking it, but my clothes seem to fit about the same. Nothing big that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or do you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yeah. People at work have noticed it too. Sometimes I move real slow, like my boots are full of wet cement—takes me forever just to pick up a board or get going. Other times I’m the opposite, pacing around the shop, rubbing my hands, but not actually getting anything done.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty or worthless about yourself or your situation?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I keep going back over every appointment, every change in my wife’s health, and I tell myself I should’ve caught something sooner. Like if I’d been a better husband, maybe I could’ve protected her somehow. It’s hard not to blame myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or anything like that. I get stuck in my own head sometimes, but not like hearing things other people can’t hear.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that don't seem to make sense or are not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I get worried and I overthink things, but I don’t believe things that aren’t real, as far as I can tell. Nothing like that has stood out to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things around you are not real or that you're detached from the world or yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve felt that. I get overwhelmed sometimes, but I don’t feel like the world isn’t real or like I’m outside myself watching things happen.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s been a real problem. I’ll be looking at blueprints I’ve read my whole life, and it’s like the lines just blur together in my head. I know I should understand it right away, but I can’t focus, and I end up asking the younger guys to repeat things I should’ve caught the first time.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has the sadness been constant?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty constant. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest, like everything in the house has lost its color. I have to really hold it together so I don’t break down in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two weeks where you felt okay for at least a couple of days?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I might get through parts of a day, but I haven’t felt okay for a couple days straight. Most mornings I wake up with that heavy, empty feeling, like the color’s gone out of the house, and I’m just trying not to fall apart in front of my wife.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last six months where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s been bad for about six months now. It didn’t really come and go so much as it just kept dragging on, and at some point it stopped feeling like a rough week or normal stress. It’s been hanging over me for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or beliefs that are not based in reality?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve been feeling really low, but I don’t think I’ve believed anything that wasn’t real. If you mean hearing or seeing things or being convinced of something strange, no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any experiences where you heard or saw things that others couldn't?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or seen things that other people couldn’t see. Nothing like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I used to look forward to salsa nights, church gatherings, cooking for the neighbors… even just stepping into my shop and smelling the wood. Now I don’t really want to do any of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself or wanting to be dead?\n\nPatient: Yes… I have. I don’t have a plan or anything, but I do think sometimes that maybe it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And sometimes I catch myself thinking my family might be better off without me. That scares me to say out loud.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move?\n\nPatient: Yeah. My energy is really low. Even if I sit down for a while or get a full night in bed, I still feel worn out—like I’ve been framing a roof all day.
